# EconByte 1.1.4 — content accuracy audit (Phase 12)

Date: 2026-09-14 · Branch `claude/econbyte-114-pro` · Owner order: "drag a fine tooth comb through all of the new content".

## Scope

Everything added since 1.1.3 shipped, plus the two 1.1.3 packs:

- `EconByte/Resources/courses-v1.json`: 3 courses × 6 lessons. Every block was read (paragraphs, callouts, key terms, diagram captions against what `DiagramView.swift` draws, chart titles, captions, markers and the synthetic data, quiz questions, correct answers, distractors, explanations, takeaways, sources).
- `EconByte/Resources/packs-v1.json`: 6 packs × 4 topics × 8 cards = 192 cards, plus pack and topic summaries. Checked title, definition, example, `claim` metadata and source.
- `EconByte/Resources/brief-sample.json`: every figure re-read against the official BLS, Federal Reserve and BEA pages.
- In-app copy that makes factual claims: Pro paywall, Unlock All paywall, pack rows, lesson lock, Pro tab, Home course row, brief teaser and banner, the Settings "Sources & editorial policy" page, and the `EconByte.storekit` product descriptions.

## Method

1. Each factual claim was checked against the page it cites, opened on 2026-09-14 (WebFetch; `curl` with a browser user agent for loc.gov). Where the page could not support the claim, the text was corrected to what the page says, or the claim was re-cited to another page on the approved host list.
2. Numbers, signs, units, periods, seasonal-adjustment notes, dates, names, definitions and causal wording were compared one by one. Every `observation` claim was checked as a reading reproducible from its page. Every `illustration` claim's arithmetic was recomputed.
3. Every quiz was re-solved independently. A distractor that is arguably correct counts as a defect (`ambiguous`).
4. Charts were checked in two ways:
   - A script confirmed that all 19 charts have a `dataNote` starting "Synthetic", that every candle is valid (high ≥ max(open, close), low ≤ min(open, close)), that x values increase, and that every marker sits on a data point.
   - Each chart was also checked by hand to confirm it shows the pattern its lesson names.
5. Tone: educational, with no advice, predictions or named securities, and balanced where evidence is contested. Also checked: no stale wording, no year after 2026, US spelling, plain English, and no duplicates of the 120 core cards.
6. The work was split across sub-agents by pack and by course, each writing a findings file with exact old → new edits. The phase lead reviewed every proposed edit and re-checked the highest-impact numbers personally. Edits were applied with an old-value check and in the files' own JSON format (minimal diff). `scripts/validate_content.mjs` (packs, courses, brief) and the `EconByteTests` suite were then re-run.

Severity key:
- `factual`: wrong number, date, name, attribution or mechanism.
- `outdated`: superseded vintage.
- `ambiguous`: imprecise enough to mislead, or a quiz with more than one defensible answer.
- `unsourced`: the claim is not supported by the cited page.
- `tone`: advice, prediction or loaded framing.
- `typo` / `spelling`: includes British spellings.
- `duplicate`: repeats a core or other pack card.

In the tables, "correct" means the item was checked and needed no change.

## Most important corrections (lead's judgment)

1. **Daily Brief, Employment Situation "meaning"**: the text said a steady unemployment rate meant no change in "the share of people looking for work". The unemployment rate is the jobless share of the labor force, and participation actually rose to 61.6%. Rewritten. Every CPI, PPI, jobs, FOMC and schedule figure in the brief was re-read on the official release and needed no change.
2. **stg-005**: said inflation was below 5% in early 1976, near 7% in March 1979 and 9% at the end of 1979. BLS CPI-U shows 6.7%, 10.1% and 13.3% (FRED CPIAUCNS, checked by the lead). Removed.
3. **stg-006**: the Fed essay's 6.1% (early 1982) and 3.7% (1983) reproduce on neither the PCE nor the core PCE index; 3.7% is the CPI. Both removed; the measure is now named for the verified 7.7% and 11.6%.
4. **chn-004**: China's 2025 primary-industry value added is 9.33 trillion yuan (NBS 93,347 hundred million), not 9.93.
5. **cmp-005**: the Rule of 72 error was described backwards. The rule overstates doubling time below about 8% and understates it above.
6. **ia-01 chart**: the caption said selling in month 17 would be a loss, but the synthetic path is at 109.51, above its start of 100. Caption and markers now point to months 4 and 9, the only months below 100.
7. **bry-02 duration chart**: the 30-year bars were inconsistent with the 10-year bars. Recomputed for a 4% semiannual bond: −15.5% / +19.7%.
8. **rpc-04**: Lo–Mamaysky–Wang (NBER w7613) tested multi-day patterns, not hammer, doji or engulfing candles, and reported possible practical value. The lesson now states what the paper tested and presents the evidence in both directions (plus w5505, w20721).
9. **Attribution errors**:
   - pln-005: "astronomical quantities of everything" described the military's view, not the War Production Board chairman's own.
   - mix-006: the 1935 NLRB had 3 members (5 from 1947).
   - dep-007: deposit insurance became permanent in 1935, not July 1934.
   - mkt-005: the statute says "reasonable and just", not "just and reasonable".
10. **Outdated figures**:
    - csc-007: FTC 2025 fraud data ($15.9 billion, 3 million reports) replaces 2024.
    - csc-002: the $14.50 report-price cap is gone; permanent free weekly reports described instead.
    - pay-008: the stale 2024 wage base, which conflicted with the 2026 figure in pay-004, is removed.
    - stg-007: late-1982 unemployment is no longer the postwar high (14.8% in April 2020).
11. **Duplicates**:
    - Removed and replaced: cmp-002 and cmp-003 (repeated rr-003 and inf-001). New cards: "Regular Deposits Over Time" (Investor.gov calculator; illustrative math checked) and "A Guaranteed Doubling" (TreasuryDirect EE bonds).
    - Rewritten: byg-008 as Home Equity (repeated hm-006); cmp-008 as The National Deposit Rate (repeated ir-003); gfc-007 around the 2008–2010 limit change (repeated personal-pack cards); pln-004 (repeated sd-006).
12. **Copy**:
    - The Pro paywall used the wrong bonds course title.
    - The brief teaser's "Every U.S. business day" was removed, because the server job that publishes daily briefs is not built. Restore it when the job ships.
    - Settings now names international organizations on the brief source list.

## Notes on the counts

- 25 of the fixed items change only source metadata. Many cards had stamped the check date (2026-09-14) as the page's publication date, or used a server Last-Modified header where the page states its own date. These are counted as `ambiguous`. Setting them aside, 121 items had substantive defects.
- 146 items changed in total: 144 fixed, plus 2 removed and replaced under the same ids so every topic keeps 8 cards.
- The lead corrected sub-agent proposals before applying them in six places:
  - bry-02: rounding, −15.5 not −15.4.
  - rpc-01: dropped an unverified reference to the diagram's color.
  - gfc-007: removed an unsourced generalization; datePrecision set to stated-on-page.
  - stg-006: removed the mixed-measure figures instead of labeling them PCE.
  - byg-008: stated the 30-year term the arithmetic assumes.
  - csc-002: fixed a dangling "same website".

## Verification after the fixes

- `node scripts/validate_content.mjs courses|packs|brief … --net`: 0 errors and 0 warnings for each. URLs: courses 35/35, packs 166/166, brief 7/7, all final 200.
- `EconByteTests` on iPhone 17 (iOS 26.5): 328 executed, 0 failures, 10 skipped. The skips are `StoreKitSessionTests`, which skip by design on this Mac (Phase 11). Log: `~/dudley-evidence-retention/econbyte/1.1.4/phase12/unit-tests-final.log`.
- Mechanical chart check over all 19 charts: `dataNote` starts "Synthetic"; candles are valid; x values increase; markers sit on data points.
- No layout code changed. The edits are content strings and three slightly longer copy strings, so no device install was needed.

## Could not verify, or left as is

- Nothing is marked `unverified`. Investor.gov, sec.gov and loc.gov refuse the fetch tool, so those pages were read with a browser-user-agent `curl`. The FRED CSV endpoint was unreachable from the build Mac, so FRED's plain-text data pages were used instead.
- Two cited Fed History essays contain errors of their own: dep-007 (deposit insurance permanence) and stg-006 (mixed inflation measures). The cards now follow the FDIC and FRED data, and still cite the essays for the facts the essays get right.
- The ECB "Our money" page still lists Bulgaria's euro adoption as scheduled, while the ECB press release confirms entry on January 1, 2026. eur-001 states the confirmed fact.
- Kept deliberately:
  - "Harmonised Index of Consumer Prices" (eur-004), the official proper name.
  - "Two per cent inflation target", an ECB document title.
  - tsb-002…004 cite a St. Louis Fed essay whose title contains "Bitcoin"; no brand name appears in the card text.
- Conceptual overlaps that add new data or a new angle were noted, not flagged as duplicates. For example: usa-002/gdp-006, usa-003/lm-001, wlf-005/rec-005, rr-007/ia-05, fdv-005/ia-02.
- Oldest sources still in use: cr-005 (CFPB, reviewed 2020-08-28) and cr-003 (2022-12-28). Their content still matches the pages.
- Owner decision: when the Daily Brief server job ships, restore the "every U.S. business day" cadence in the brief teaser and the methodology text.

## Phase 13 additions

Date: 2026-09-14/15 · Branch `claude/econbyte-114-pro` · Owner order: grow the content by 50% ("100 units → 150") with the same audit gate.

### What was added
- Core curriculum: 60 cards (15 topics × 4; ids `-009`…`-012`); every topic now has 12. Free/paid split unchanged.
- Packs: 96 cards (6 packs × 4 topics × 4); every pack topic now has 12 (48 per pack).
- Courses: 9 lessons (ia-07…09, rpc-07…09, bry-07…09), each with ≥5 blocks, a chart and/or one of five new diagrams (`rebalance-bands`, `trendline-anchors`, `base-rate-grid`, `credit-spread-stack`, `breakeven-split`) and one quiz. Course summaries and durations updated.
- Daily Brief: five bundled samples (2026-09-08, 09, 10, 11, 14), replacing the single `brief-sample.json`.

### Method
1. Thirteen writer lanes (3 core, 6 packs, 3 courses, 1 briefs) wrote from primary sources against the Phase 12 checklist (mixed measures, caption vs data, attribution and statute wording, approximation direction, vintages, essays with their own errors, duplicates, US spelling, advice tone, unverifiable claims) and logged every claim.
2. A different agent independently verified every lane: it opened every cited page itself, recomputed every illustration and chart, re-solved every quiz, and swept for duplicates across the whole app and all new lanes. Hosts that refuse this Mac (SSA, OECD, bls.gov, sec.gov, some FRED and IMF endpoints) were read with WebFetch or a browser-user-agent curl, or re-checked from the writers' saved page copies; each entry's note says which.
3. The lead reviewed every proposed edit before applying it and made these calls:
   - **Cross-lane duplicates** (caught by verification, not by the validator):
     - rec-010 repeated ei-009 (jobless claims).
     - tt-010 repeated the shipped tax-007 (tariff incidence).
     - usa-012 repeated tt-012 (the net international investment position).
     - mix-010 repeated gfc-011 (the Fannie Mae and Freddie Mac conservatorship).
     - The four removed slots got new cards, verified in a second round: Chicago Fed National Activity Index, Anti-Dumping Duties, Output per Hour, Government as a Buyer.
   - **Variety:** three of the four new Risk & Return cards were about leverage. rr-011 (maintenance margin) became "Bear Markets and Recovery Math", which a second agent verified.
   - **Brief calendar:** the August Monthly Treasury Statement was released on Sep 11, not Sep 14 (8th workday; PDF Last-Modified). It moved into the Sep 11 brief. The Sep 14 brief was rebuilt around the Federal Reserve H.10 release of Sep 14, and its repeated CPI item was removed. A second verifier re-checked both briefs, and the FOMC schedule line now follows the Fed calendar's own wording.
   - **Lesson length:** the lessons ran long. Verifier trims that cut repetition were applied; no claim that a quiz or takeaway depends on was cut.
   - **Not applied:** retitles of the shipped cards usa-003 and usa-004, whose duplicate-title warnings date from Phase 12. That is outside this phase's scope.
4. Verification dates. New items record the pass that actually verified them. The validators now accept any pass between each catalog's floor and its `verifiedOn` (core floor 2026-08-30; packs and courses 2026-09-14), so the 432 shipped items were not re-stamped.

The tables below use the lead's final disposition. "fixed" means the verifier's exact edit was applied, and "removed" means the item was cut, with a replacement where a card slot needed one. The severity key is the same as above.
